### [2026-08-05T18:08:26.670Z] USER

is deterministic better or wors than another option? 


### [2026-08-05T18:08:50.103Z] ASSISTANT ()

**Deterministic is better here — and it's a deliberate design choice in this tool.** The pipeline is actually a hybrid:

| Step | Approach | Why |
|---|---|---|
| File enumeration, language/category detection, line counts | **Deterministic** (`scan-project.mjs`) | Pure rule-lookup — no judgment needed. Deterministic = fast, cheap, reproducible, zero hallucinated/missed files |
| Import resolution | **Deterministic** (`extract-import-map.mjs` via tree-sitter) | Same input → same edges, and it enables the **fingerprint baseline** in Phase 7 (auto-update compares structural fingerprints across commits — only works if structure extraction is reproducible) |
| Narrative synthesis (name, description, frameworks) | **LLM** (what I did inline) | Requires reading README/manifest and inferring meaning |
| Per-file nodes/edges, layers, tour | **LLM** (subagents in Phases 2–5) | Requires semantic judgment — summaries, tags, architecture interpretation |

**The alternative** (older design): the LLM *wrote a Node script on every run* to walk the tree and classify files. That was slower, billed LLM rates for pure rule lookups, and produced inconsistent inventories run-to-run.

**The one real tradeoff:** deterministic tree-sitter import extraction can miss imports an LLM would catch (dynamic `require()`, non-standard resolvers, unusual syntax). That's mitigated two ways — the import map only *boosts* cross-batch edges (file-analyzer agents still read actual source and emit `imports` edges themselves), and batching uses it only for community detection. Also, we saw warnings that swift/scala grammars weren't loaded — irrelevant here since this repo is ~85% JavaScript.

So: deterministic for *facts* (inventory, imports, fingerprints), LLM for *meaning* (analysis, layers, tour). The deterministic half being deterministic is what makes incremental updates and auto-update possible at all.
